#2995. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.â¯â¯

Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System

20th Street and Constitution Avenue N.W., Washington, DC 20551

#2993. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guidance; Extension of Comment Period

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

Agencies seek comment on proposed third-party risk management guidance and issue statement on community bank engagement with core service providers

#2991. ‘Fun to see’ Karl-Anthony Towns evolve during Knicks championship run: ex-coach

MINNEAPOLIS — Karl-Anthony Towns' old coach has seen the full journey.

Chris Finch heard all the criticism Towns constantly received during his Timberwolves tenure.

"Particularly when he started his playoff journey," Finch said before the Knicks' 102-99 win over the Timberwolves on Thursday night, "how much flak he had gotten."

Towns returned to Minnesota for the third time since the blockbuster trade with the Knicks two years ago, recording 22 points, 12 rebounds and four assists in the win. It was his first time returning as an NBA champion.

Timberwolves head coach Chris Finch speaks on the sideline as guard Bones Hyland looks on during a Nov. 16, 2026, in Minneapolis. AP Photo/Ellen Schmidt

He is no longer the oft-criticized star many saw as an underperformer and contract albatross. Towns now is playing some of the best basketball of his career after playing a central role in the Knicks' championship. He has shed a lot of the narratives that plagued him as a pro.

"It was fun to see him at the heart of it, too," Finch said. "Especially as they were kind of tweaking their offense going along and all of his skills came to the surface through that."

Towns called it "bittersweet" returning as a champion, having wanted to deliver a title to the organization with which he spent his first nine years.

Karl-Anthony Towns shoots over center Rudy Gobert during the Knicks' 102-99 win over the Timberwolves on Nov. 19, 2026, in Minneapolis. AP Photo/Ellen Schmidt

"I still have so much love for this place," Towns said. "It doesn't go away. I think it's actually grown as I've been away. I think that even for me and my dad, we were just driving around the city and just going to places. We went to eat at a spot we love to go here. It always hits home. This is the last place that I got to play basketball with my mom. I got to see the kids be young before now. Jolani, my niece, who was in my Rookie of the Year photo actually in here, she was a baby, and now she's in high school. It's a different part of life, but it's a part of life that I really enjoy."

Head coach Mike Brown said his team's hustle allowed it to escape with a win.

"The thing that saved us was winning the 50-50 ballgame," Brown said. "We were 13-for-14 in that area, and that was huge for us because we had trouble scoring in the fourth."

#2990. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2987. NBA Rookie Of The Year Power Rankings: Who’s Emerging As The Early Favorite?

A lot has changed in the Rookie Of The Year race since the first Power Rankings we did back in the first week of the NBA's 2026-27 Season. AJ Dybantsa came back and made his stellar debut with the Washington Wizards, Yaxel Lendeborg has had to carry a bigger burden without Stephen Curry, and Cameron Boozer even grabbed the second-youngest triple-double ever.

Now, how does this change the ROTY race as we hit the first month of competition so far? There've been some incredible revelations, as Cleveland's Meleek Thomas is scoring in double digits and becoming a 6MOTY candidate, while Darius Acuff Jr. keeps averaging an incredible 26.2 PPG, and Darryn Peterson is becoming increasingly more comfortable scoring.

With so many rookies playing well already, picking a clear favorite isn't that easy anymore, especially with some players getting more minutes because of injuries and others starting to find their place on their teams. So, let's take a look at the updated top 10 rookies in the league, who's moving up, and who has the best chances of winning the award this season.

The Milwaukee Bucks have been terrible to start the season, sitting at 1-15 after another loss on Thursday, but Brayden Burries has been one of the few good things happening for them. The No. 10 pick is putting up 10.4 points, 5.3 rebounds, and 2.3 assists per game, shooting 42.6% from the field, and he's already getting a good amount of minutes for a team that needs more help from its young players.

What's been surprising is his rebounding, because Burries is only 6-foot-4 and still ranks sixth among all rookies with 5.3 rebounds per game. He's also playing just 22.5 minutes, and his numbers per 36 minutes would be around 15.5 points, 8.8 rebounds, and 3.9 assists. Those are good numbers for a guard who wasn't expected to play this much right away.

Burries also had 13 points and eight rebounds against the Hawks earlier this month, and he's already had several games with six or more rebounds. The Bucks have been changing their rotation a lot, so he's getting more chances to play against starters and show what he can do instead of spending most of his time on the bench.

His 30.5% shooting from three still needs work, and that's one of the main reasons why he isn't higher on this list. But Milwaukee doesn't have many reasons to celebrate right now, and Burries should keep getting more minutes as the season goes on.

Keaton Wagler has been getting more chances with the LA Clippers recently, and while his shooting hasn't been great, he's already showing some good things in his first NBA season. The No. 5 pick is putting up 12.1 points, 3.4 rebounds, and 3.0 assists per game, shooting 36.4% from the field and 35.9% from three, while playing 22.4 minutes a night.

What's interesting about Wagler is how much he does in those minutes. As NBA.com's Rookie Ladder pointed out, his numbers per 36 minutes are 19.2 points, 5.3 rebounds, and 5.2 assists, while he's also getting to the free-throw line a lot. He's not afraid to attack the basket, and he's already getting some chances to create his own shots despite playing with several veterans.

He recently had 14 points in only 18 minutes against the Thunder, and he's also been doing a good job finding teammates when he gets the ball. The Clippers haven't had a great start to the season, so giving Wagler more minutes could be a good way to see what they have in their rookie guard.

Still, shooting 36.4% from the field isn't good enough if he wants to compete with the top players in this class. He's making some threes and showing he can score in different ways, but he needs to be more efficient. I think ninth place is fair for now, although he could move up if he starts getting closer to 15 points per game.

One of the biggest surprises in this rookie class has been Meleek Thomas, who wasn't even selected in the first round but is already playing an important role for the Cleveland Cavaliers. The No. 34 pick is scoring 13.4 points per game, with 2.8 rebounds, 1.6 assists, and 1.2 steals, shooting an impressive 47.5% from the field and 44.2% from three.

And those shooting numbers are even better when we look at how many threes he's making. Thomas is hitting 2.6 three-pointers per game, the second-most among rookies, only behind Darius Acuff Jr., and he's doing that in just 23.8 minutes. He's also posting a 60.2% true shooting mark, which puts him among the most efficient scorers in this rookie class.

Thomas recently scored 20 points against the Mavericks, making eight of his 17 shots, and he's now had five straight games with double-digit points. He's also started three games for the Cavaliers, which is pretty impressive for someone who wasn't expected to play such a big role on a team trying to compete in the East.

I think Thomas deserves more attention, especially because he's doing all this without taking as many shots as the top rookies. He's also becoming a possible Sixth Man of the Year candidate, and if he keeps shooting over 40.0% from three, Cleveland will have a hard time keeping him out of the rotation. For now, he's one of the best surprises of the 2026 NBA Draft.

Even without scoring 20 points every night, the Oklahoma City Thunder have found a very good center with the No. 12 pick in Aday Mara. The 7-foot-3 rookie is putting up 9.2 points, 6.5 rebounds, 1.9 assists, and 1.7 blocks per game, shooting 63.5% from the field, and he's doing all that while playing just 22.0 minutes per game.

Mara is already leading all rookies in blocks, while also ranking fourth in rebounds, and his 64.2% true shooting is one of the best marks in this league. He's not getting many shots compared to the other players on this list, but he's making most of them, and Oklahoma City doesn't really need him to score much more than that.

One of his best games came against the Kings, when he finished with 16 points, 13 rebounds, and three assists, including eight offensive rebounds. That's a lot of extra chances for a Thunder team that already has several good scorers, and Mara is also showing that he can pass the ball pretty well for a center.

I think he could become one of the best players from this draft, especially if he keeps improving next to Chet Holmgren. OKC already has a strong defense, and having another big man who can block shots and get rebounds is a good addition. Mara probably won't win Rookie of the Year with these scoring numbers, but his impact has been much bigger than his 9.2 PPG suggests.

Caleb Wilson looked like one of the main favorites for Rookie of the Year before getting hurt, and his first few games were among the best we've seen from anyone in this class. The Chicago Bulls forward is still putting up 18.9 points, 6.2 rebounds, 1.9 assists, and 1.8 steals per game, shooting 42.0% from the field and 32.4% from three.

Wilson was the third-leading rookie scorer before his injury, and even now, only Acuff and Lendeborg are scoring more than him. He's also leading all rookies in steals, while his 2.3 three-pointers per game rank third. Those numbers are incredible for someone who was only selected with the No. 4 pick and wasn't expected to carry so much of the offense right away.

He also joined Allen Iverson as the only rookies in NBA history to record at least 150 points, 15 three-pointers, and 10 steals in their first seven games. But Wilson suffered a fractured right hand and hasn't played since, with the Bulls expecting him to remain out until at least mid-December.

And Chicago has actually been playing better without him recently, including a 137-107 win over the Nets. That doesn't mean Wilson was making the team worse, but it will be interesting to see what happens when he returns.

For me, Wilson would be in the top five if he was healthy, and maybe even higher. But missing several weeks is a problem when Acuff, Lendeborg, and the other rookies keep playing every night and improving their numbers.

AJ Dybantsa had to wait a little longer to make his NBA debut because of an ankle injury, but the No. 1 pick is finally starting to look more comfortable with the Washington Wizards. He's putting up 16.3 points, 6.6 rebounds, 2.3 assists, and 1.3 steals per game, shooting 41.6% from the field, and his last few games have been much better than his first appearances.

Dybantsa just had his best game of the season against the Raptors, finishing with 23 points and 12 rebounds on 9-for-17 shooting, while helping Washington win 103-99 and end an eight-game losing streak. It was also his first NBA double-double, and he's starting to get more chances to score with the ball in his hands.

He's already had two games with at least 20 points, five rebounds, and three steals, while his 6.6 rebounds rank third among rookies. Dybantsa is also playing 33.7 minutes per game, the second-most in this class, only behind Acuff, so the Wizards are clearly giving him a lot of time to improve.

His biggest problem is still his outside shooting, because 21.3% from three isn't good enough for a player who's taking so many shots. But he's getting to the basket, grabbing rebounds, and already showing some good things on defense.

I think Dybantsa could move up this ranking pretty soon, especially if he starts making more threes. He missed some games early in the season, but with Washington giving him such a big role, he'll have plenty of chances to catch the other rookies.

Darryn Peterson has been moving up the Rookie of the Year race almost every week, and the Utah Jazz are getting some great games from their No. 2 pick. He's putting up 16.5 points, 4.1 rebounds, and 3.6 assists per game, shooting 40.4% from the field and 36.1% from three, but his recent numbers are even better than that.

In fact, Peterson has been scoring 20.3 points per game over his last seven appearances, adding 4.6 rebounds and 3.4 assists, and he's also had five straight games with at least 20 points. That made him the first teenager in Jazz history to reach that mark, and the second-youngest player in NBA history to do it, only behind Anthony Edwards.

His best game came against the Rockets on November 13, when he scored a career-high 28 points with seven rebounds and four three-pointers. During his five-game streak of 20-point performances, Peterson shot 47.3% from the field and 38.6% from three, showing a huge improvement from his first games.

The Jazz have been dealing with injuries to Lauri Markkanen, Jaren Jackson Jr., and Keyonte George, so Peterson has been getting more shots and more chances to run the offense. Utah still has one of the worst records in the West, but at least its rookie guard is playing better almost every night.

And I think Peterson has a very good chance to finish in the top three. He's already making 2.2 threes per game, ranking fourth among rookies, and his scoring has been getting closer to the top players on this list. If he keeps putting up 20 points per game, he could become one of the main favorites pretty soon.

Cameron Boozer has been one of the most complete rookies in the league, and the Memphis Grizzlies are getting a lot more than just scoring from him. The rookie forward is putting up 16.6 points, 9.2 rebounds, and 5.3 assists per game, shooting 41.7% from the field and 32.2% from three, while helping Memphis become one of the better teams in the West.

Boozer is leading all rookies in rebounds, ranks second in assists behind Acuff, and is also one of the top scorers in this class. That's an incredible combination for a 19-year-old forward, especially when he's playing just 30.8 minutes per game.

He also made history in his fifth NBA game, recording 18 points, 12 rebounds, and 12 assists against the Trail Blazers to become the second-youngest player ever to get a triple-double. That game showed how much he can do with the ball, and his passing has been one of the biggest surprises of his rookie season.

And Boozer has been scoring more lately too. He recently had a career-high 29 points against the Wizards, making seven three-pointers while adding seven rebounds and seven assists. He also shot 48.6% from the field over a recent five-game stretch, which is a good improvement after struggling with his shot earlier in the season.

I think Boozer has a strong case to be even higher, especially because he's helping a winning team while doing a little bit of everything. But Lendeborg has been scoring more efficiently, and Acuff's offensive numbers are still too good to ignore. For now, Boozer stays third, although he's getting closer to the top two.

As one of the biggest surprises of the season, nobody expected the No. 11 pick to become such an important player for the Golden State Warriors this quickly, but Yaxel Lendeborg is scoring 19.7 points per game, with 7.3 rebounds and 3.7 assists, shooting 45.5% from the field and 37.2% from three, and he's been getting a much bigger role since Curry got hurt.

Lendeborg is second among rookies in scoring and rebounding, while also ranking fifth in assists, and his 57.4% true shooting is better than most of the players scoring over 15 points per game. He's also already had eight games with at least 20 points, which is impressive for someone who wasn't expected to be one of Golden State's main offensive options.

He recently scored 25 points and grabbed 10 rebounds against the Rockets, before adding 28 points, eight rebounds, and three assists against the Pelicans. He also had a stretch of four games with at least 23 points in five appearances, showing that he can keep scoring even when the Warriors aren't playing well.

But Curry's injury has made things harder. In his first four games without him, Lendeborg shot only 34.2% from the field and 19.0% from three, while taking almost five more shots per game. Defenses are paying much more attention to him now, and he's still getting used to having such a big role.

Lendeborg was also No. 1 in NBA.com's latest Rookie Ladder, and I think there's a good argument for putting him first, but with Acuff scoring more than 26 points every night, I'm keeping Lendeborg second for now. If his shooting gets better again and the Warriors start winning, he could easily become the favorite.

Darius Acuff Jr. has been putting up some incredible numbers since his first NBA game, and I still think he deserves the No. 1 spot in the Rookie of the Year race. The Sacramento Kings guard is scoring 26.2 points per game, with 3.6 rebounds and 7.0 assists, shooting 41.6% from the field and 34.3% from three, and he's already one of the highest-scoring players in the entire league.

Yes, one could argue that Acuff is taking a lot of shots (22.6 FGA, third in the entire league), and also that he may not involve his teammates as much, with Domantas Sabonis putting up just 13.7 PPG, but the ROTY is less about winning and more about performance, and he has been filling the stat sheet despite the efficiency woes.

Acuff is leading all rookies in both points and assists, and nobody else is even close to his scoring average. Lendeborg is second with 19.7 points, which means Acuff is scoring 6.5 more points per game while also getting more assists than anyone in this class.

He also had one of the best rookie performances of the season against the Grizzlies on October 31, scoring a career-high 38 points with five rebounds and five assists, including nine three-pointers. That came after he had already scored 33 points in his second NBA game, so he didn't need much time to start putting up huge numbers.

And he's still doing it almost every night. Acuff just scored 36 points against the Suns on Wednesday, making 11 of his 22 shots and six of his 11 three-pointers, while adding four rebounds and six assists. He's also scoring 28.1 points per game over his last 10 appearances, which is even better than his season average.

His 3.3 three-pointers per game lead all rookies, and he's already had 10 games with at least 20 points. Of course, the Kings have been terrible, and Acuff's 41.6% shooting from the field could be better, but he's also playing 36.6 minutes per game and taking a lot of difficult shots because Sacramento needs him to score.

For me, that's enough to keep him first. Boozer has been more complete, Lendeborg has been more efficient, and Peterson is getting closer, but nobody in this rookie class is scoring like Acuff. If he finishes the season with something close to 26 points and seven assists per game, I think it'll be very difficult to give the award to someone else.

#2986. Celtics mailbag: Worried about Robinson? White's 3-PT shooting and more

Celtics mailbag: Worried about Robinson? White's 3-PT shooting and more originally appeared on NBC Sports Boston

When we ripped open the Celtics Mailbag for the first time last week, optimism was high. Boston had just swept a four-game road trip and owned the best record in the Eastern Conference. The questions centered largely on whether Boston was a legitimate title contender after a fast start to the 2026-27 campaign.

The first losing streak of the new season has a way of reeling things in a bit. It's not exactly time to break out the panic meters after stumbles against Orlando and Denver, but let's just say there are a few lingering areas of concern.

Let's start with one concern that was there even when the Celtics were winning as we tackle a worry-themed collection of questions in this week's bag:

How worried should we be that Mitchell Robinson still hasn't turned a corner in Boston?

BOSTON, MASSACHUSETTS – OCTOBER 23: Mitchell Robinson #4 of the Boston Celtics celebrates after blocking a shot from Og Anunoby #8 of the New York Knicks during the Celtics Home Opener at TD Garden on October 23, 2026 in Boston, Massachusetts. The Celtics defeat the Knicks 107-103.

By now you've surely seen the stat. The Celtics have outscored opponents by 157 points this season, but Robinson is a team-worst minus-37 in his 246 minutes on the floor through 15 games. Plus/Minus can be a flukey stat but, somehow, the eye test suggests it might actually be even worse than the numbers indicate.

What's wild is that Robinson has spent more than 70 percent of his floor time playing alongside Jayson Tatum and yet the team has been outscored by 8.5 points per 100 possessions in those 174 minutes. Boston owns a +20 net rating in Tatum's 303 minutes without Robinson.

Some growing pains could be expected. The Celtics demand a lot of their big men and it can take time for players to settle into the system. Robinson has admitted at times that his conditioning is not where it needs to be, which is slightly frustrating given Boston's hefty offseason investment and the fact that a full month of the regular season is already in the rearview.

Robinson put together one of his more encouraging halves of the season in Denver on Wednesday night — and he even made consecutive free throws — but then produced a less-enthralling five-minute stretch in the third quarter as the Celtics came unglued. Boston leaned heavily on 19-year-old rookie Chris Cenac Jr. in the fourth frame instead of going back to Robinson.

We didn't like Robinson's energy in the third quarter, whether it was being unprepared for the ball when Tatum got blitzed, or glued to the ground on available rebounds on the defensive end.

It's much too early to make any wholesale declarations about the Robinson signing but undeniably both the team and player need to evaluate the reasons for his slow start. Do the Celtics tone down how much screening they ask him to do on the offensive end and keep him closer to the rim, where his offensive rebounding can tilt possessions? Does Robinson simply need to amp up his energy level and embrace all that the Celtics are tossing on his plate?

The fact that Neemias Queta has produced some of the best on/off splits in the NBA has made it easier to let Robinson ride the rollercoaster. Luka Garza struggled out of the gates last season but eventually settled into his role. Alas, Garza leaned into his natural motor and Robinson needs to crank up his own.

Robinson does feel vital to Boston's ceiling. The Celtics need Robinson to be a defensive anchor against the league's best bigs. They need his typical offensive rebounding rates to dominate the margins on the offensive end.

The Celtics don't have a lot of concerns despite their recent stumbles and getting Robinson activated feels like priority No. 1.

How worried should we be about Derrick White's jumper?

Feb 4, 2026; Houston, Texas, USA; Boston Celtics guard Derrick White (9) shoots the ball as Houston Rockets guard Amen Thompson (1) defends during the second quarter at Toyota Center. Mandatory Credit: Troy Taormina-Imagn Images

Even as White's shot has defied him for a season-plus now, we still expect him to make everything he hoists in the fourth quarter. So it was jarring that, back in his native Colorado and with his parents watching behind the Boston bench, White misfired on his last five shots against Denver. We were *certain* his 3-point look from the right wing with 27 seconds to play would drop.

It didn't. White is now shooting 30.6 percent beyond the 3-point arc through 15 games, even lower than last season's output (32.7) and well below his career mark (35.9).

White has played 99 games since the start of the 2025-26 season and has made 250 of the 780 3-point attempts — regular season and postseason combined — that he's hoisted in that 13-month span. He's reeled in his attempts from deep this year, dropping down to 5.7 per game this season after 8.3 per game last year.

The concerning part: White is getting better looks this year. Last season, his expected 3-point percentage was 35.3 percent, so he finished about 2.6 percent below expected overall. This season, NBA tracking has him at 36.3 expected on all 3-point attempts — right around league average, and his career mark — but he's nearly 6 percent below expected on tracked attempts.

The positive spin: White continues to impact winning. The Celtics have a +12.9 net rating in his 448 minutes on the court. Boston is 7-0 when White registers multiple STOCKS (steals and blocks). White is averaging 13.6 potential assists per 100 possessions, ranking in the 94th percentile for his position. His 1.2 offensive fouls drawn per 100 possessions ranks in the 96th percentile. He's still doing a lot of the things that make him a winning player.

Getting those shooting numbers up is likewise vital to Boston's overall ceiling.

How worried should we be that Denver delivered a blueprint for slowing down Tatum and the Celtics offense?

Oct 30, 2026; Boston, Massachusetts, USA; Boston Celtics head coach Joe Mazzulla talks with forward Jayson Tatum (0) from the sideline as they take on the Chicago Bulls at TD Garden. Mandatory Credit: David Butler II-Imagn Images

Tatum and the Celtics certainly didn't respond well when the Nuggets got blitz-happy in the second half. Tatum did the right thing trying to move the basketball when an extra body shuffled his way but it also led to a series of turnovers that allowed Denver to erase its 17-point deficit.

Tatum's teammates didn't aid the cause, though. Tatum finished with a team-high 16 potential assists but that translated to a mere three actual assists and six assist points overall. By comparison, White's 14 potential assists translated to eight actual assists and 20 assist points. Boston players need to shoot better when Tatum is creating open looks with the attention he draws. We'll have our eyes on how Boston responds if Sacramento starts sending blitzers on Saturday night.

Foul trouble didn't help matters, either, for Boston on Wednesday with Paul George playing just 11 minutes, 21 seconds in the second half. Tatum and White were a combined 3-of-14 shooting after the intermission. Boston was 2-of-19 (10.5 percent) on all second-half 3s.

The 11 second-half turnovers was still the most jarring number. Boston can't get sloppy when teams blitz. Tatum's potential assists number suggests he still often made the right play, the Celtics simply didn't make the opponent pay. And they compounded matters with the giveaways.

How worried should we be that opposing defenses will help off Jordan Walsh when he's running with the starting group?

The White-Walsh-George-Tatum combo is Boston's most frequent 4-man combo (113 minutes) this season and owns a +20 net rating in their floor time. That's encouraging. Still, it's clear that opponents will cheat away from Walsh and then challenge him to shoot if it means getting the ball out of the hands of Tatum or George. He's made strides with his offensive confidence but the offense grinds a bit when he's hesitant.

Walsh did not play in the second half in Denver. The Celtics have no shortage of options with that fifth spot. We've only seen 14 minutes with the core starters (White, George, Tatum, Queta) alongside Sam Hauser, but that group has a 150 offensive rating and +64.8 net rating overall. We'd probably like to see how Hauser holds up, defensively, in longer stretches with that group. Size wise, it's a super intriguing mix, though speedy guards could stress that 5-man pairing.

We've also only seen five minutes of Baylor Scheierman with that core group. Boston's offensive rating dips to 109.5 in the 22 minutes that Hugo Gonzalez has played with the White-George-Tatum-Queta combo.

How worried should the rest of the league be about Chris Cenac Jr. being this impactful as a 19-year-old rookie?

We'll continue to scream it: The biggest silver lining in the two recent losses was getting to throw Cenac Jr. into the fourth-quarter fire against both Orlando and Denver, and for him to hold up well in both situations.

The Celtics can trot out a bunch of double-big combos with Cenac Jr. able to pair with any of the other bigs on the roster. Cenac Jr. uses his length, even against shifty guards like Jamal Murray, and doesn't look overwhelmed against switching. Opponents are shooting 38.1 percent against him (8-of-21 overall), or seven percent below expected output. Those numbers plummet to 28.6 percent shooting inside six feet, or 34.5 percent below expected, albeit on a small sample (2-of-7 overall).

If Robinson finds his footing, we suspect Cenac Jr. will still see much of his development up north with Maine in the G-League. Reps are important at his age. But what a luxury to be able to throw him into NBA crunch-time situations as a teenager and have him look confident. His length and athleticism can be game-changers for this team.

#2985. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

For release at 10:00 a.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released results from a survey of senior financial officers at banks about their strategies and practices for managing reserve balances. The Senior Financial Officer Survey is used by the Board to obtain information about banks' reserve balance management strategies and practices, their expectations around the size of their balance sheets and deposit balances their activity in money markets, and their views on other matters, such as operations on weekends and holidays.

The survey was conducted in collaboration with the Federal Reserve Bank of New York between September 18, 2026, and September 28, 2026, and includes responses from banks that held around three-fourths of total banking system reserve balances at the time of the survey.

#2981. Yaxel Lendeborg's bold spirit being tested by his unwillingness to impose will

Yaxel Lendeborg's bold spirit being tested by his unwillingness to impose will originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

In the first half of the final game of the Warriors' disastrous five-game road trip, Thursday night in Memphis, the curtain was raised on cinema the likes of which they never again care to watch.

Their offense was an oil spill, with turnovers coming in waves, giving the Grizzlies 21 of their 55 points. Their defense was shockingly lenient, allowing the Grizzlies wide-open 3-point shots with the kind of comfort typically associated with a robe, slippers and a pipe.

Yet the most concerning observation for the Warriors today and beyond was Yaxel Lendeborg's unwillingness to impose his will.

Lendeborg, a rookie but the most dynamic Warrior on the floor, played 17 of the quietest minutes of his promising career. He scored four points before intermission, fewer than four of his teammates. He made zero field goals, which is unsatisfying but not alarming. Most startling was that he attempted only three shots from the field.

The top scorer on Golden State's active roster, whose flame roared so loudly only 10 days ago, left his bravado in the locker room – or locked it inside his head. The force behind his best work was on recess in the first 24 minutes.

To be fair, there were many culprits behind a 126-98 loss that sent the Warriors home with their first 0-5 road trip in more than four years. Once Stephen Curry was injured in the road opener, feebleness cascaded over the likes of Draymond Green, Brandin Podziemski, De'Anthony Melton, Gui Santos, Al Horford and Georges Niang.

Golden State's starting lineup in Memphis – Charles Bassey, Santos, Lendeborg, Melton and Podziemski – combined for seven first-half field goals. Bassey, effectively the team's third-string center, was responsible for all but three of the buckets.

Lendeborg's performance in this game and, moreover, his output on the entire road trip, is most troubling because of what he represents. Selected No. 11 overall in the 2026 draft, he is Golden State's first lottery pick with apparent cornerstone potential since Klay Thompson in 2011. Yaxel is the most viable reason for a kernel of optimism about the franchise's post-Curry future.

Lendeborg was more intentional in the second half, shooting 3-of-8 from the field, including nailing his only triple, adding six assists and four rebounds. He finished with 11 points, eight rebounds and seven assists. After halftime, he looked as if he was trying to gather scattered bits of vitality.

If this game had come two weeks earlier, Lendeborg likely would have glared at Memphis rookie Cam Boozer, selected eight picks earlier in the draft and a staunch competitor in the Rookie of the Year race, and breathed fire. Armed himself for battle.

While it's unfair to expect and unreasonable to believe Lendeborg should be the driving force of the Curry-less Warriors, it's within his ability to sharpen his focus, which spent most of the game floating about FedExForum. He fell asleep so soundly on defense during one Memphis possession – granted, the same could be said of most of his teammates – that coach Steve Kerr called a timeout and addressed him coming off the floor.

Focus and effort top the list of things an athlete can control. Yaxel's focus clearly impacted his effort on this night and during this trip. He was tentative with his shot, despite having a green light. His most demonstrative moment was a physical hallelujah, arms raised, after his third-quarter triple.

He deserved that moment after a road trip in which he averaged 16 points (5.4 below his previous season average) on 33.3-percent shooting from the field, including 20 percent beyond the arc.  His average plus-minus was minus-28.2

Lendeborg's superpower is his determination to make himself matter. To let his teammates know he'll do whatever it takes to win while also showing opponents that he's not afraid to engage physically or even verbally if so moved.

That Yax and his prodigious promise made barely a cameo appearance Thursday, and the same could be said of most of a road trip he and the Warriors never again wish to experience.

Lendeborg's journey to the NBA and the Warriors suggests those five games are not the first time he has been slapped by failure. He always recovered in the past, and that spirit needs to reemerge.

#2979. Who will play third base for the Royals now?

The Royals have traded away third baseman Maikel Garcia to replenish their pitching depth. While that may address the rotation, the departure of their All-Star leaves a major hole at third. For a team that also needs to address center field and perhaps left field, that's another significant need to add to an already lengthy offseason shopping list.

Who could the Royals turn to at third base? There are several potential options available through trades or free agency, ranging from established veterans to younger players who could fit into the team's long-term plans.

Nick Loftin would be the next man up on the depth chart. The 28-year-old former first-round pick had a breakout season, getting an opportunity when Garcia missed time with injuries. He hit .240/.322/.388 with eight home runs in 118 games, good for a league-average OPS+ of 100. He had a solid 9.6 percent walk rate and a low 17.1 percent strikeout rate. He was not far behind Garcia for the lowest swing rate and best contact rate on the team. Loftin played all over the field, and while he had some defensive miscues at second, he was solid at third with some highlight–reel plays. He finished at +6 Defensive Runs Saved and was a Gold Glove finalist at the position.

The Royals may not see his 2026 performance as sustainable, and may prefer him in a utility role. J.J. Picollo praised Loftin for his defense, but still insisted that the trade left a "void in our offense."

"But knowing he can play second, he can play left, he can move around gives us the flexibility to try to determine what's the bat that we (have) to go out and try to acquire to replace the production of Maikel Garcia."

With that in mind, the Royals may seek to look outside the organization for their next third baseman.

Alec Bohm is the name that has come up the most as a potential replacement. The Royals reportedly had interest in the Nebraska native before. The former third-overall pick had a sensational rookie season, but has had a mixed track record since then. He hit 20 home runs in 2023, and 11 more heading in to the All-Star break in 2024, where he was a participant in the Home Run Derby. But he hit just four home runs after the break, and followed it up with an 11-home run season. This year he had his lowest OPS+ since 2021, hitting .249/.315/.383 with 15 home runs, and was below replacement level according to Baseball Reference. The 30-year-old hits from the right side, which the Royals' lineup needs, and perhaps their coaches can reenergize his bat.

Jazz Chisholm Jr. is probably the top infield free agent with MLB experience, and the Yankees may be ready to move on from him. The 28-year-old was an All-Star in 2025 with a career-high 31 home runs, but had the rare contract year regression in 2026. This year he hit .231/.312/.399 with 19 home runs, although with a career-high 41 steals. He has played mostly second base and center field in his career, but he started 73 games at third for the Yankees from 2024-25, albeit with poor results. Chisholm has a history of beefing with the Royals, and it would be ironic if he replaced Garcia, one of the players he's had issues with. Chisholm's upside could land him a lucrative deal, but his mental gaffes and a lackluster season may reduce demand for his services. He could end up signing a short-term deal in an effort to rebuild his market appeal.

Teruaki Sato could be one of the most intriguing options on the market, although he would also be one of the most expensive. The 27-year-old Japanese star is coming off a monster season with the Hanshin Tigers, winning the Central League Triple Crown with a .306/.383/.609 line and 39 home runs. Sato is a left-handed hitter with tremendous power, and he won a Gold Glove at third in Japan. There are some questions about how his contact rate and defense will translate to MLB, but his offensive upside is considerable. The Yankees, Mets, Dodgers and Phillies have all been linked to him, so the Royals would face some stiff competition in luring an Asian player to the Midwest. Still, if they really want to make a big splash, Sato would be the player to target.

Eugenio Suárez might be a stretch as a third baseman at this point in his career. He still has great power, adding 26 home runs last year to give him 351 in his career. But his overall hitting numbers fell to .212/.307/.433, his worst line since 2023. He rated as a poor defender at third the last few seasons, and has been playing more at DH. But he still has good pop and is just a year removed from a 52-home run All-Star season in 2025. The Royals could add him to the lineup and hide his defense by having him split time between third with Loftin and DH. But at age 35, he is in the twilight of his career, and the Royals already have one aging star they have to transition to DH.

Si-Hwan Roh is another international option for the Royals to consider, as the 25-year-old South Korean third baseman is available through the posting system this offseason. Roh spent this year with the Hanwha Eagles, where he hit .283/.360/.505 with 29 home runs in 127 games. He has been one of the KBO's most productive power hitters in recent years, ranking second in the league with 116 home runs since 2023. The Yankees, Mariners, Phillies and Pirates have reportedly shown interest, but he is likely to command a lower deal than Sato.

Others: Mauricio Dubón, Yoán Moncada, Luis Rengifo, Edmundo Sosa

Brett Baty was previously connected to the Royals in trade rumors, but after career numbers in 2025, his numbers fell off sharply this year. He hit just .238/.311/.374 with 11 home runs in 143 games, although he did post good walk rates and was able to elevate the ball more than in the past. He has been unplayable against lefties, which limits his utility for the Royals. The Mets have played him all over the field, so he could benefit from sticking to one position. Baty has good power potential and is still young enough to turn things around, but he would be a risky proposition with his track record.

Nolan Gorman could end up on the free agent list if he is non-tendered by the Cardinals. The former first-round pick hit 27 home runs in 2023, but has been a major disappointment since then. He bottomed out this year, hitting just .184/.272/.308 with nine home runs in 85 games, earning a demotion to Triple-A. But the left-handed hitter is just 26 years old with several controllable years left and 30+ home run potential if he can get back on track. His defense is passable at third, and despite his struggles, he doesn't have much of a platoon split.

Colt Keith is a 25-year-old left-handed hitter with the Tigers, who hit .258/.322/.406 with 10 home runs in 140 games this year. He hasn't developed the power some scouts projected, but he makes consistent contact with low strikeout rates. His bat is a liability against lefties and he has had mixed results defensively at third. He has experience at second and first base as well. The Tigers have committed to him with a very club-friendly long-term deal through 2029 that includes three team options, so they may not be that inclined to move him.

Royce Lewis is also a non-tender candidate with the Twins after hitting just .227/.287/.380 with 15 home runs in 125 games. He put up solid offensive numbers early in his career, but has gotten worse each season. His defense has also been a bit mixed, and the Twins moved him to first base mid-season. Lewis is a former #1 overall pick who was a 2.4-rWAR player in 2023, but may need a change of scenery. The 27-year-old would not be eligible for free agency until 2029.

Coby Mayo may be available as the Orioles are open to revamping their roster. He emerged to hit 24 home runs in his first full season with the Orioles, with a line of .228/.297/.461 in 132 games. The 24-year-old has the right-handed bat the Royals covet with good power potential. He is a free swinger with a strikeout rate over 30 percent this year, and his defense was average. It may take a lot to acquire a player so early in his career, but the Orioles don't seem particularly wedded to guys they drafted.

Isaac Paredes could be one of the more appealing trade targets for the Royals if the Astros are willing to move him. The 27-year-old is coming off a productive 2026 season, hitting .262/.360/.447 with 25 home runs, and was worth 4.1 rWAR. Paredes is one of the biggest pull hitters in baseball, and he actually would have hit more home runs had he played all his games at Kauffman Stadium. He had a healthy 10.8 percent walk rate and the second-lowest strikeout rate of any hitter with at least 20 home runs. He will earn $13.35 million next year before becoming a free agent, so he is a short-term rental.

Casey Schmitt had a career year with the Giants, hitting 22 home runs with a line of .271/.301/.483 before a knee injury ended his season in July. He can play all over the field, but was a solid defender at third in place of the injured Matt Chapman. With Chapman returning, the Giants could sell high on Schmitt to address pitching. The 27-year-old has several controllable years left and has shown decent offensive number prior to this year. He is also a free swinger who had the lowest walk rate in baseball among hitters with 400 plate appearances, and he could be exposed with more playing time.

Matt Shaw is another first-round pick who has had an underwhelming MLB performance so far and could be on the move. He was a 3 rWAR player in his rookie season in 2025, hitting .226/.295/.394 with 13 home runs, but missed two months this season with a hand injury. The Alex Bregman signing forced him into more of a utility role and he might be penciled in the Cubs outfield this year. But the team could look to move him in return for some pitching or catching help.

Jordan Westburg is another Orioles infielder that could be available, although he comes with some injury concerns. The right-handed hitter was an All-Star in 2024, hitting .265/.312/.481 with 18 home runs, and he followed that up with a solid 2025. But he missed all of this season after suffering a UCL tear that required Tommy John surgery. He is expected to be ready for next season, but may find playing time more difficult in a crowded Orioles infield. Baltimore may not want to "sell low" on a player, but if they are open to ideas, he could be a solid cost-controlled player for the Royals for several years.

Ben Williamson was acquired a year ago by the Rays from the Mariners, but with Junior Caminero entrenched at third, Carson Williams ready to take over at short, and Richie Palacios and Taylor Walls still in the mix, Williamson could be on the move again. The 26-year-old is a defense-first infielder, hitting just .245/.298/.314 in 581 plate appearances over the last two years. He was +8 in Defensive Runs Saved at third base for the Mariners in 2025 in limited action, while the Rays played him more at second. He has never hit for any kind of power, but he did post better walk rates in the minors.

Others: Cody Freeman, Zack Gelof, Charles McAdoo, Josh Smith, Tim Tawa, Jared Triolo

#2972. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2968. Indianapolis Colts release QB Daniel Jones

Mike Garafolo of the NFL Network is reporting that the Indianapolis Colts released quarterback Daniel Jones on Friday. Jones has played very poorly since returning from the torn Achilles that ended his 2025 season, prompting the release

As pretty much everyone will remember, the New York Giants drafted Jones out of Duke with the sixth overall pick in the 2019 NFL Draft. The Colts will now turn to Anthony Richardson, and he'll be the quarterback for whom the Giants will need to game plan when the two teams face off next week.

The Giants' decision to draft Jones was something of a surprise on draft night, as the 2019 quarterback class was not seen as a strong one at all. Jones would go on to replace Giants' legend Eli Manning as the starting quarterback for the Giants game against the Tampa Bay Buccaneers in Week 3 of that year.

Though Jones won his first game as a starter, his time with the Giants was tumultuous, to say the least. There were flashes of the potential that caused GM Dave Gettleman to fall in "full bloom love" with Jones in his Senior Bowl MVP performance, but there were also far too many turnovers and injuries. The Giants had hoped Jones turned the corner when the team surprisingly made the playoffs in 2022, and rewarded him with a rich 4-year, $160 million contract in the spring of 2023. Unfortunately, the injuries and poor play returned that season Jones was ultimately benched following the Giants' 2024 loss to the Carolina Panthers in Munich.

Jones requested his release after being benched and landed with the Minnesota Vikings. He backing up Kirk Cousins for that team's own playoff run. He then signed a two-year, $88 million contract with the Colts as a free agent in 2025.

Jones initially played well for the Colts, taking advantage of the talents of RB Jonathan Taylor, TE Tyler Warren, and WR Alec Pierce in the Colts' play-action and RPO-heavy offense. However, his play fell off beginning with the Colts loss to the Pittsburgh Steelers and his season was ended early by a torn Achilles.

It's anyone's guess where Jones will land next, however he should be comfortable in the meantime. Jones has, so far, made a combined $172.8 million as a quarterback for the Giants, Vikings, and Colts.

#2964. Thomas Müller calls out ‘laissez-faire’ MLS format as Germany legend debates lack of relegation

Germany and Bayern Munich icon Thomas Müller has now called Major League Soccer, and more specifically the Vancouver Whitecaps, home since August 2025.

During his time in Canada, the 37-year-old FIFA World Cup winner has won a Canadian Championship and reached last season's MLS Cup game, losing 3-1 to Lionel Messi's Inter Miami.

Now, ahead of this season's Playoffs, for which the Whitecaps qualified as the Western Conference's winners, Müller has given his insight into the pros and cons of the lack of relegation within the current MLS format compared to the top European leagues.

There's something special about Wrexham - and we proved it in two games

Full UEFA Nations League draw, including tantalizing quarter-finals for England and Spain

Müller's Whitecaps are returning to the Playoffs as Western Conference champions -Credit:Minas Panagiotakis/Getty Images

Speaking to Global News, the two-time UEFA Champions League winner said, "There is no relegation. There is no big pressure. There is, 'Oh yeah, we do a rebuild.' Nobody's happy to lose, but it's totally different when you fight for getting relegated.

"Maybe you lose budget for the next year, the whole club, you lose people working at the club when the budget changes. It's totally different for fans, the pressure. The players have maybe no contracts for next year when you go down."

According to Müller, this lack of severe consequences has a knock-on effect not only on the front office, but also on the players.

"The playing style changes. When you play a bottom-of-the-league team in Europe, they fight for their lives," he said. "And sometimes I have the feeling because of the system, the playing style is a little bit more, let's say, optimistic or careless defensively. So a little bit, just a little bit more, the French would say, laissez-faire.

"That's maybe good for seeing some offensive, nice offensive plays when the games are opening up. When I compare it, I see sometimes really, really good individual actions in players. What I miss sometimes is maybe the consistency. The good and the bad, the gap is bigger.

To listen to the latest episode of 'All Out Soccer' - CLICK HERE

"But the good is, we can compare it with golf. Even a 10 handicapper has sometimes shots, a professional couldn't do better. So it's maybe the free kick, top-corner free kick, but the bad shots of a professional would be never that bad. And that's a little bit, sometimes it's more like the attention of the players to be focused over 90 minutes. That's what I see sometimes, their lack."

While the prospect of MLS switching to a promotion/relegation system has been brought up before, the likelihood of it happening is exceedingly small, as, back in August, commissioner-elect Larry Berg said that switching formats simply wasn't on the cards, as per The Athletic. That being said, the league is said to be looking to nullify some of the more 'meaningless' games by increasing the playoff field from 16 to 20 teams.

The implementation of any movement up or down the MLS 'pyramid' may not be on the horizon, but what is inbound is a major schedule change. As of 2027, the league will be switching from its usual spring-through-fall format to follow in the footsteps of most major leagues, instead adopting the summer-through-spring schedule.

In order to accommodate this change, the MLS will also be holding a one-off Sprint Season between February and May 2027, for which Müller has extended his contract. The 2027-2028 season will be the first played within the new format, and it should allow for better alignment with European transfer windows and international breaks.
